# Bhagavad Gītā

`src:bhagavad-gita` · `sourced` · confidence high

**Alternate titles:** Gītā, Śrīmad Bhagavad Gītā, Gītopaniṣad, Harigītā (as the Nārāyaṇīya refers to Kṛṣṇa's teaching)
**Original title:** भगवद्गीता
**Language:** Sanskrit
**Family:** vedic
**Lineages:** [Epic teaching (the Mahābhārata's Sāṃkhya-Yoga and Mokṣadharma milieu)](../lineages/epic-teaching.md), [Early Bhāgavata devotion (the Nārāyaṇīya / Sātvata–Ekāntika dharma)](../lineages/bhagavata-early.md), [Vedānta (Uttara Mīmāṃsā)](../lineages/vedanta.md)
**Genre:** gītā (dialogue); smṛti-prasthāna of Vedānta
**Part of:** [Bhīṣmaparvan](bhismaparvan.md)
**Location in parent:** Bhīṣmaparvan adhyāyas 23–40 (Critical Edition) = 25–42 (vulgate)
**Authors:** 
  - [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md) — role: revealer; attribution: traditional
  - [Vyāsa (Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana)](../teachers/vyasa.md) — role: author; attribution: traditional
**Attribution:** tradition: Spoken by Kṛṣṇa (the Lord) to Arjuna, heard and relayed to Dhṛtarāṣṭra by Sañjaya through the divine sight granted by Vyāsa (18.75), and set in the Mahābhārata by Vyāsa. The chapter colophons call it an Upaniṣad, a knowledge of Brahman (brahmavidyā) and a treatise on yoga (yogaśāstra).; scholarly: An integral part of the Bhīṣmaparvan whose unity and date are debated; many scholars see a single composition drawing on Upaniṣadic, Sāṃkhya-Yoga and Bhāgavata materials, others posit layers.; confidence: moderate
**Dates:** Tradition's account: spoken on the first day of the Kurukṣetra war at the end of the Dvāpara yuga (before the Kali yuga, traditionally reckoned from 3102 BCE); Scholarly account: c. 2nd c. BCE – 2nd c. CE (estimates range from the 5th c. BCE to the 3rd c. CE); (confidence moderate)
**Structure:** 18 chapters, 700 verses in the vulgate and the Critical Edition (a verse at the head of ch. 13 in some printed editions makes 701). The Kashmir recension (commented on by Abhinavagupta and Rājānaka Rāmakaṇṭha) has additional verses and many variant readings. A verse found in some manuscripts (the 'Gītāmāna') speaks of 745 verses (620 by Kṛṣṇa, 57 by Arjuna, 67 by Sañjaya, 1 by Dhṛtarāṣṭra); it is not in the Critical Edition.
**Availability:** digitized-original

The dialogue in which Kṛṣṇa, as charioteer, instructs Arjuna on the battlefield of Kurukṣetra after Arjuna refuses to fight: the imperishable self, action without attachment to its fruits (karma-yoga), knowledge, meditation and devotion to the Lord as paths, the Lord's two natures, his descents and his universal form, the field and its knower, the three guṇas, the divine and demonic endowments, the threefold faith, and renunciation, ending in the call to take refuge in him alone (18.66).
**Editions / translations:** 
  - kind: original; name: Bhīṣmaparvan 23–40 in the Mahābhārata Critical Edition, ed. S. K. Belvalkar, BORI (1945–47); licence: print
  - kind: original; name: gita/gita JSON corpus (Sanskrit text, transliteration, several commentaries); licence: Unlicense; url: https://github.com/gita/gita
  - kind: original; name: Gita Press, Gorakhpur editions (vulgate; with Śaṅkara's and other commentaries); licence: print
  - kind: original; name: F. O. Schrader, The Kashmir Recension of the Bhagavadgītā (Stuttgart, 1930); licence: print
**Commentaries on this text:** [Anāsakti Yoga of M. K. Gandhi](anasakti-yoga.md), [Bhagavad-gītā As It Is](bhagavad-gita-as-it-is.md), [Bhagavadgītā Vīraśaiva-bhāṣya (T. G. Siddappārādhya)](bhagavadgita-virasaiva-bhasya.md), [Essays on the Gita of Sri Aurobindo](essays-on-the-gita.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Bhāskara](gita-bhasya-bhaskara.md), [Gītābhāṣya (Madhva)](gita-bhasya-madhva.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Rāmānuja](gita-bhasya-ramanuja.md), [Gītābhāṣya of Śaṅkara](gita-bhasya-sankara.md), [Gītābhūṣaṇa of Baladeva Vidyābhūṣaṇa](gita-bhusana.md), [Gītā Rahasya (Karmayogaśāstra) of Bāl Gaṅgādhar Tilak](gita-rahasya.md), [Gītātātparyanirṇaya](gita-tatparya-nirnaya.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha](gitarthasangraha.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha of Abhinavagupta](gitarthasangraha-abhinavagupta.md), [Gītārthasaṅgraha of Yāmuna](gitarthasangraha-yamuna.md), [Gītāvivṛti](gitavivrti.md), [Gūḍhārthadīpikā](gudharthadipika.md), [Jñāneśvarī (Bhāvārthadīpikā)](jnanesvari.md), [Kathā-Gītā (of Bhaṭṭadeva)](katha-gita-bhattadeva.md), [Sārārthavarṣiṇī](sarartha-varsini.md), [Sārārthavarṣiṇī of Viśvanātha Cakravartin](sararthavarsini.md), [Sarvatobhadra of Rājānaka Rāmakaṇṭha](sarvatobhadra-ramakantha.md), [Subodhinī of Śrīdhara Svāmin](subodhini-sridhara.md), [Tattvaprakāśikā of Keśava Kāśmīrī Bhaṭṭa](tattvaprakasika-kesava-kasmiri.md), [Tattvaprakāśikā (Gītā commentary of Keśava Kāśmīrin)](tattvaprakasika-kesava-kasmirin.md), [Tattvārthadīpanibandha of Vallabha](tattvartha-dipa-nibandha.md)

## Teachings (447: text-verified 165, sourced 282)

### 1.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Dhṛtarāṣṭra asks Sañjaya: in the field of dharma (dharmakṣetra), Kurukṣetra, assembled and eager to fight, what did my people (māmakāḥ) and the sons of Pāṇḍu (pāṇḍavāḥ) do?

> dharmakṣetre kurukṣetre samavetā yuyutsavaḥ|
māmakāḥ pāṇḍavāścaiva kimakurvata sañjaya

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative, teacher-transmission_

terms: [dharma](../terms/dharma.md) · teachers: [Dhṛtarāṣṭra](../teachers/dhrtarastra.md), [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Pāṇḍu](../teachers/pandu.md)

### 1.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Sañjaya: having seen the army of the Pāṇḍavas drawn up in battle array, King Duryodhana then approached the teacher (ācārya) and spoke these words.

> dṛṣṭvā tu pāṇḍavānīkaṃ vyūḍhaṃ duryodhanastadā|
ācāryamupasaṅgamya rājā vacanamabravīt

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

teachers: [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Duryodhana](../teachers/duryodhana.md), [Droṇa](../teachers/drona.md)

### 1.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana to the teacher: behold, teacher, this great army of the sons of Pāṇḍu, arrayed by the son of Drupada, your intelligent disciple (śiṣya).

> paśyaitāṃ pāṇḍuputrāṇāmācārya mahatīṃ camūm|
vyūḍhāṃ drupadaputreṇa tava śiṣyeṇa dhīmatā

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

terms: [śiṣya](../terms/sisya.md) · teachers: [Duryodhana](../teachers/duryodhana.md), [Droṇa](../teachers/drona.md), [Dhṛṣṭadyumna](../teachers/dhrstadyumna.md), [Drupada](../teachers/drupada.md), [Pāṇḍu](../teachers/pandu.md)

### 1.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana: here are heroes, great archers, equal in battle to Bhīma and Arjuna — Yuyudhāna, Virāṭa, and Drupada the great chariot-warrior (mahāratha);

> atra śūrā maheṣvāsā bhīmārjunasamā yudhi|
yuyudhāno virāṭaśca drupadaśca mahārathaḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana continues the list of the Pāṇḍava warriors: Dhṛṣṭaketu, Cekitāna and the valiant king of Kāśī (kāśirāja); Purujit, Kuntibhoja and Śaibya, a bull among men.

> dhṛṣṭaketuścekitānaḥ kāśirājaśca vīryavān|
purujitkuntibhojaśca śaibyaśca narapuṅgavaḥ

`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana: but know, best of the twice-born (dvijottama), those who are most distinguished among us, the leaders of my army; I name them to you for your information.

> asmākaṃ tu viśiṣṭā ye tānnibodha dvijottama|
nāyakā mama sainyasya saṃjñārthaṃ tānbravīmi te

### 1.8 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Duryodhana names the leaders of his own army: yourself (Droṇa), Bhīṣma, Karṇa and Kṛpa, victorious in battle; Aśvatthāman, Vikarṇa, and likewise the son of Somadatta (saumadatti).

> bhavānbhīṣmaśca karṇaśca kṛpaśca samitiñjayaḥ|
aśvatthāmā vikarṇaśca saumadattistathaiva ca

### 1.10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-1-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Duryodhana compares the two forces: 'aparyāpta' is that force of ours protected by Bhīṣma, while 'paryāpta' is this force of theirs protected by Bhīma.

> aparyāptaṃ tadasmākaṃ balaṃ bhīṣmābhirakṣitam|
paryāptaṃ tvidameteṣāṃ balaṃ bhīmābhirakṣitam

> tasya saṃjanayanharṣaṃ kuruvṛddhaḥ pitāmahaḥ|

> yotsyamānānavekṣe'haṃ ya ete'tra samāgatāḥ|

### 2.1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Sañjaya: to him, thus overcome by pity (kṛpā), his eyes filled with tears and troubled, despairing (viṣīdantam), Madhusūdana spoke these words:

> taṃ tathā kṛpayā'viṣṭamaśrupūrṇākulekṣaṇam|
viṣīdantamidaṃ vākyamuvāca madhusūdanaḥ

_level: unmarked · standpoint: experiential · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative_

terms: [kṛpā](../terms/krpa.md), [viṣāda](../terms/visada.md) · obstacles: [Despondency (viṣāda)](../obstacles/visada.md) · teachers: [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: from where has this faintness (kaśmala) come upon you at this critical moment (viṣame), Arjuna? It is not practised by the noble (anārya-juṣṭa), does not lead to heaven (asvargya), and brings disgrace (akīrti-kara).

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
kutastvā kaśmalamidaṃ viṣame samupasthitam|
anāryajuṣṭamasvargyamakīrtikaramarjuna

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

terms: [kaśmala](../terms/kasmala.md), [svarga](../terms/svarga.md) · obstacles: [Faintness, dejection (kaśmala)](../obstacles/kasmala.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md) · disputes: [Should Arjuna fight the battle in which his elders, teachers and kinsmen stand on the other side?](../debates/bhagavad-gita-arjuna-should-fight.md)

### 2.3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: do not yield to unmanliness (klaibya), Pārtha; it does not befit you. Cast off this petty weakness of heart (hṛdaya-daurbalya) and stand up, scorcher of foes!

> klaibyaṃ mā sma gamaḥ pārtha naitattvayyupapadyate|
kṣudraṃ hṛdayadaurbalyaṃ tyaktvottiṣṭha parantapa

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: how shall I, Madhusūdana, fight back with arrows in battle against Bhīṣma and Droṇa, who are worthy of worship (pūjārha), slayer of enemies?

> kathaṃ bhīṣmamahaṃ saṃkhye droṇaṃ ca madhusūdana|
iṣubhiḥ pratiyotsyāmi pūjārhāvarisūdana

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.5 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Arjuna: better in this world to eat even alms-food (bhaikṣya) than to kill these venerable (mahānubhāva) gurus; for having killed the gurus — 'arthakāmān' — I would enjoy, here in this very world (ihaiva), pleasures smeared with blood.

> gurūnahatvā hi mahānubhāvān
śreyo bhoktuṃ bhaikṣyamapīha loke|
hatvārthakāmāṃstu gurūnihaiva
bhuñjīya bhogān rudhirapradigdhān

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: nor do we know which is weightier (garīyas) for us — whether we should conquer or they should conquer us. The very sons of Dhṛtarāṣṭra, having killed whom we would not wish to live, stand facing us.

> na caitadvidmaḥ kataranno garīyo
yadvā jayema yadi vā no jayeyuḥ|
yāneva hatvā na jijīviṣāma
ste'vasthitāḥ pramukhe dhārtarāṣṭrāḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: ethics_

### 2.7 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: my very nature struck by the fault of pitiable weakness (kārpaṇya-doṣa), my mind bewildered about dharma (dharma-saṃmūḍha-cetāḥ), I ask you: tell me decisively what is better (śreyas). I am your disciple (śiṣya); instruct me, who have taken refuge in you (tvāṃ prapannam).

> kārpaṇyadoṣopahatasvabhāvaḥ
pṛcchāmi tvāṃ dharmasaṃmūḍhacetāḥ|
yacchreyaḥ syānniśicataṃ brūhi tanme
śiṣyaste'haṃ śādhi māṃ tvāṃ prapannam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: beginner · types: teacher-transmission, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: for I do not see what would drive away this grief (śoka) of mine that dries up the senses (indriya), even if I were to obtain on earth an unrivalled, prosperous kingdom, or even lordship over the gods.

> na hi prapaśyāmi mamāpanudyā
dyacchokamucchoṣaṇamindriyāṇām|
avāpya bhūmāvasapatnamṛddham
rājyaṃ surāṇāmapi cādhipatyam

### 2.11 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: you have grieved for those who should not be grieved for, and yet you speak words of wisdom (prajñāvāda). The wise (paṇḍita) grieve neither for those whose life has gone nor for those whose life has not gone.

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
aśocyānanvaśocastvaṃ prajñāvādāṃśca bhāṣase|
gatāsūnagatāsūṃśca nānuśocanti paṇḍitāḥ

### 2.11-30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11-30"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The teaching on the self: the wise grieve neither for the living nor for the dead; the embodied self was never non-existent and will never cease; it passes through the bodily stages and into another body; sense-contacts are transient and to be endured; the unreal has no being and the real never ceases; the self is unborn, eternal, not slain when the body is slain, not cut, burned, wetted or dried, unmanifest and unthinkable; therefore one should not grieve for any being.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: substance · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, death-dying_

terms: [ātman](../terms/atman.md), [dehin](../terms/dehin.md) · concepts: [The self](../concepts/the-self.md)

_Superseded by [2.11](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-2-11)_

Kṛṣṇa: as in this body the embodied one (dehin) passes through childhood, youth and old age, so too there is the attaining of another body; the steadfast one (dhīra) is not bewildered by this.

### 2.14 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: the contacts of the senses (mātrā-sparśa), son of Kuntī, give cold and heat, pleasure and pain; they come and go and are impermanent (anitya). Endure them (titikṣasva), Bhārata.

> mātrāsparśāstu kaunteya śītoṣṇasukhaduḥkhadāḥ|
āgamāpāyino'nityāstāṃstitikṣasva bhārata

### 2.14-15 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-14-15"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The contacts of the senses with their objects, giving cold and heat, pleasure and pain, come and go and are impermanent; endure them. The person whom these do not disturb, the same in pleasure and pain, the steady one, is fit for immortality.

### 2.16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: of what is not (asat — the non-existent or unreal) there is no being (bhāva); of what is (sat — the existent or real) there is no non-being (abhāva). The conclusion (anta) about both of these has been seen by those who see the truth (tattvadarśin).

> nāsato vidyate bhāvo nābhāvo vidyate sataḥ|
ubhayorapi dṛṣṭo'ntastvanayostattvadarśibhiḥ

### 2.20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: it is not born, nor does it ever die; it has not come to be, nor will it come to be again (or: having been, it will not again cease to be). Unborn (aja), eternal (nitya), everlasting (śāśvata), primeval (purāṇa), it is not slain when the body is slain.

> na jāyate mriyate vā kadāci
nnāyaṃ bhūtvā bhavitā vā na bhūyaḥ|
ajo nityaḥ śāśvato'yaṃ purāṇo
na hanyate hanyamāne śarīre

### 2.22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: as a man casts off worn-out garments and takes other, new ones, so the embodied one (dehin) casts off worn-out bodies and passes into other, new ones.

### 2.42-44 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-42-44"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

### 2.54 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Arjuna: what is the description (bhāṣā) of one whose wisdom is steady (sthita-prajña), who is established in samādhi (samādhi-stha), Keśava? How does one of steady thought (sthita-dhī) speak, how does he sit, how does he move?

> sthitaprajñasya kā bhāṣā samādhisthasya keśava|

_level: unmarked · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [sthitadhī](../terms/sthitadhi.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.54-72 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54-72"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña): asked how such a one speaks, sits and walks, Kṛṣṇa describes one who has given up all desires and is content in the self by the self; unperturbed in sorrow, without longing in pleasure, free of passion, fear and anger; unattached everywhere; withdrawing the senses from their objects as a tortoise its limbs; for whom even the taste for objects ceases on seeing the supreme; who, restraining the senses, sits intent on the Lord; who moves among objects without attraction and aversion and attains serenity; who is awake in what is night for all beings; who, like the ocean filled by rivers, is unmoved by desires entering him; who lives without longing, without 'mine' and without ego — this is the brāhmī sthiti, and one established in it even at death attains brahmanirvāṇa.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [brāhmī sthiti](../terms/brahmi-sthiti.md), [brahmanirvāṇa](../terms/brahma-nirvana.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md), [Liberation while living](../concepts/liberation-while-living.md)

_Superseded by [2.54](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-2-54)_

### 2.55 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-55"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: when one gives up all the desires (kāma) that have entered the mind (manas), Pārtha, content in the self alone, by the self (ātmany evātmanā tuṣṭaḥ), then he is called one of steady wisdom.

> śrī bhagavānuvāca
prajahāti yadā kāmān sarvān pārtha manogatān|
ātmanyevātmanā tuṣṭaḥ sthitaprajñastadocyate

_level: unmarked · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge · stage: realized (sthitaprajña) · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [sthitaprajña](../terms/sthitaprajna.md), [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [ātman](../terms/atman.md), [manas](../terms/manas.md) · concepts: [The person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña)](../concepts/sthitaprajna.md) · obstacles: [Desire (kāma)](../obstacles/kama.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.56 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-56"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: one whose mind is not shaken in sorrows, who has no craving (spṛhā) in pleasures, from whom passion (rāga), fear and anger (krodha) have gone — he is called a sage (muni) of steady thought.

> duḥkheṣvanudvignamanāḥ sukheṣu vigataspṛhaḥ|
vītarāgabhayakrodhaḥ sthitadhīrmunirucyate

One whose mind is not agitated in sorrows and who is free of longing in pleasures, from whom passion, fear and anger have gone, is called a sage of steady mind; one who is unattached everywhere, neither rejoicing nor hating on meeting with good or ill, has established wisdom.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: realized (sthitadhī muni) · types: consciousness-mind, ethics_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: he who is without attachment (anabhisneha) everywhere, who, meeting this or that, pleasant or unpleasant (śubhāśubha), neither rejoices nor hates — his wisdom is firmly established (prajñā pratiṣṭhitā).

Kṛṣṇa: restraining them all, let him sit yoked (yukta āsīta), intent on me (mat-para); for he whose senses are under control — his wisdom is firmly established.

> tāni sarvāṇi saṃyamya yukta āsīta matparaḥ|
vaśe hi yasyendriyāṇi tasya prajñā pratiṣṭhitā

### 2.62 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-62"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: for a person who dwells on (dhyāyataḥ) sense-objects, attachment (saṅga) to them arises; from attachment desire (kāma) is born; from desire, anger (krodha) arises;

> dhyāyato viṣayānpuṃsaḥ saṅgasteṣūpajāyate|
saṅgāt saṃjāyate kāmaḥ kāmātkrodho'bhijāyate

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [viṣaya](../terms/visaya.md), [saṅga](../terms/sanga.md), [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [krodha](../terms/krodha.md) · concepts: [From dwelling on objects to ruin (BhG 2.62–63)](../concepts/descent-from-sense-objects-to-ruin.md) · obstacles: [Attachment (saṅga)](../obstacles/sanga.md), [Desire (kāma)](../obstacles/kama.md), [Anger (krodha)](../obstacles/krodha.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.62-63 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-62-63"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

For a person dwelling on sense-objects attachment to them arises; from attachment desire is born, from desire anger; from anger comes delusion, from delusion the loss of memory, from loss of memory the destruction of understanding, and from the destruction of understanding one perishes.

> dhyāyato viṣayān puṃsaḥ saṅgas teṣūpajāyate saṅgāt saṃjāyate kāmaḥ kāmāt krodho 'bhijāyate krodhād bhavati saṃmohaḥ saṃmohāt smṛtivibhramaḥ smṛtibhraṃśād buddhināśo buddhināśāt praṇaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

terms: [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [krodha](../terms/krodha.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md) · obstacles: [The chain of ruin from dwelling on objects](../obstacles/chain-from-dwelling-on-objects.md)

_Superseded by [2.62](bhagavad-gita.md#tea-bhagavad-gita-2-62)_

### 2.63 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-63"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: from anger comes delusion (saṃmoha); from delusion, confusion of memory (smṛti-vibhrama); from the loss of memory, the destruction of understanding (buddhi-nāśa); from the destruction of understanding, one perishes.

> krodhādbhavati saṃmohaḥ saṃmohātsmṛtivibhramaḥ|
smṛtibhraṃśād buddhināśo buddhināśātpraṇaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [krodha](../terms/krodha.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md) · concepts: [From dwelling on objects to ruin (BhG 2.62–63)](../concepts/descent-from-sense-objects-to-ruin.md) · obstacles: [Anger (krodha)](../obstacles/krodha.md), [Delusion (moha)](../obstacles/moha.md) · teachers: [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md)

### 2.64 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-64"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: but one who moves among sense-objects with senses free from passion and aversion (rāga-dveṣa) and under the self's control, being self-governed (vidheyātman), attains serenity (prasāda).

> rāgadveṣaviyuktaistu viṣayānindriyaiścaran|
ātmavaśyairvidheyātmā prasādamadhigacchati

### 2.64-65 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-64-65"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who moves among objects with senses free of attraction and aversion and under the control of the self attains serenity (prasāda); in serenity all sorrows come to an end, for the understanding of the serene soon becomes steady.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

`text-verified` · confidence high

Kṛṣṇa: in serenity (prasāda) there arises for him the ending of all sorrows; for the understanding of one whose mind is serene (prasanna-cetas) quickly becomes steady.

### 2.66 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-66"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Kṛṣṇa: for one who is not yoked (ayukta) there is no understanding (buddhi), nor is there contemplation (bhāvanā) for the unyoked; for one without contemplation there is no peace (śānti), and for one without peace, where is happiness?

### 2.66-68 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-2-66-68"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

For the unyoked there is no understanding and no contemplation (bhāvanā); without contemplation no peace; for one without peace, how can there be happiness? The mind that follows the roving senses carries away one's wisdom as the wind a ship on the waters; therefore one whose senses are withdrawn from their objects has established wisdom.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

tadasya harati prajñāṃ vāyurnāvamivāmbhasi

### 3.6-7 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-6-7"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

One who restrains the organs of action but sits dwelling in mind on sense-objects is deluded and called a hypocrite; but one who controls the senses by the mind and, unattached, undertakes the yoga of action with the organs of action, excels.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action · stage: intermediate · types: practice, ethics_

### 3.10-16 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-10-16"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

In the beginning Prajāpati created beings together with sacrifice and said: by this you shall multiply; nourish the gods with it and they will nourish you; one who enjoys their gifts without offering to them is a thief; the good who eat the remains of sacrifice are freed from all faults, while those who cook only for themselves eat sin; beings come from food, food from rain, rain from sacrifice, sacrifice from action, action from Brahman (the Veda), and Brahman from the Imperishable — so the all-pervading Brahman is established in sacrifice; one who does not turn the wheel thus set in motion lives in vain.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual, action · stage: all · types: world-fate, practice, ethics_

### 3.25-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-25-26"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

As the unwise act attached to action, so the wise should act unattached, wishing to hold the world together; the wise should not unsettle the understanding of the ignorant who are attached to action, but, acting yoked, should engage them in all actions.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: action · stage: advanced · types: ethics, teacher-transmission_

### 3.36-39 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-36-39"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Arjuna asks what impels a person to sin even against his will; Kṛṣṇa answers: it is desire, it is anger, born of rajas, all-devouring and very sinful — know it as the enemy here; as fire is covered by smoke, a mirror by dust and an embryo by the womb, so knowledge is covered by it — the constant enemy of the wise in the form of desire, an insatiable fire.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

### 3.42-43 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-3-42-43"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The senses, they say, are high; higher than the senses is the mind, higher than the mind is the understanding, and higher than the understanding is he; knowing him who is beyond the understanding, steadying the self by the self, slay the enemy in the form of desire, so hard to overcome.

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind_

`sourced` · confidence high

I taught this imperishable yoga to Vivasvat; Vivasvat told it to Manu and Manu to Ikṣvāku; handed down thus in succession the royal sages knew it; by long lapse of time the yoga was lost here; that same ancient yoga I have told you today, because you are my devotee and friend — it is the highest secret.

_level: conventional · standpoint: divine · path: general · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission_

`sourced` · confidence high

### 4.25-30 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-4-25-30"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The kinds of sacrifice: some yogins offer sacrifice to the gods; others offer sacrifice by sacrifice into the fire of Brahman; some offer hearing and the other senses into the fires of restraint, others sound and the other objects into the fires of the senses; others offer all the actions of the senses and of the breath into the fire of the yoga of self-restraint kindled by knowledge; others sacrifice with material things, with austerity, with yoga, and ascetics of strict vows with study and knowledge; others, devoted to breath-control, offer the in-breath into the out-breath and the out-breath into the in-breath, restraining their courses; others, regulating food, offer breaths into breaths — all these know sacrifice and their faults are destroyed by sacrifice.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ritual · path: ritual, meditation, body-breath · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [krodha](../terms/krodha.md) · obstacles: [Desire and anger, the enemy (kāma–krodha)](../obstacles/kama-the-enemy.md)

### 5.24-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-5-24-26"></a>

One whose happiness, delight and light are within becomes Brahman and attains brahmanirvāṇa; the seers whose faults are destroyed, whose doubts are cut, who are self-controlled and delight in the welfare of all beings attain brahmanirvāṇa; for ascetics freed from desire and anger, with minds controlled and knowing the self, brahmanirvāṇa is near on every side.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

_level: bridging · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

`sourced` · confidence high

### 6.10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-10"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The yogin should constantly concentrate himself, remaining in a solitary place, alone, with mind and body controlled, without expectation and without possessions.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice_

practices: [The Gītā's meditation (dhyāna-yoga, BhG 6.10–32)](../practices/dhyana-yoga-gita.md)

### 6.10-32 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-10-32"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The method of meditation: the yogin should constantly concentrate himself, alone in a solitary place, with mind and body controlled, without expectation or possessions; set up a firm seat in a clean place, neither too high nor too low, covered with cloth, deerskin and kuśa grass; sitting there with mind one-pointed, controlling thought and senses, practise yoga for self-purification; holding body, head and neck erect and still, gazing at the tip of the nose without looking around, serene, fearless, firm in the vow of celibacy, the mind restrained and fixed on me, sit yoked and intent on me; moderate in food, sleep, recreation and effort; when the controlled mind rests in the self alone, free from longing, it is steady like a lamp in a windless place; the mind stilled by practice comes to rest, and seeing the self by the self one is content in the self, knowing the endless happiness grasped by the understanding beyond the senses; this is yoga, the unyoking from union with sorrow; withdrawing the mind gradually by a steady understanding and fixing it on the self, one should think of nothing; wherever the restless mind wanders, bring it back under control in the self; the yogin with a calm mind attains the supreme happiness of contact with Brahman, sees the self in all beings and all beings in the self, sees me everywhere, and regards the pleasure and pain of all as his own.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind, body-layers_

terms: [dhyāna-yoga](../terms/dhyana-yoga.md), [yoga](../terms/yoga.md) · practices: [The Gītā's meditation (dhyāna-yoga, BhG 6.10–32)](../practices/dhyana-yoga-gita.md)

### 6.11-12 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-6-11-12"></a>

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

`sourced` · confidence high

The unintelligent think of me, the unmanifest, as having come into manifestation, not knowing my higher, imperishable, unsurpassed being; veiled by my yoga-māyā I am not manifest to all, and this deluded world does not know me, the unborn and imperishable; I know the beings of the past, present and future, but no one knows me.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate_

### 7.27-28 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-7-27-28"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

By the delusion of the pairs of opposites arising from desire and aversion, all beings fall into delusion at birth; but people of meritorious deeds whose sin has come to an end, freed from the delusion of opposites, worship me firm in their vows.

`sourced` · confidence high

_level: bridging · standpoint: divine · path: knowledge, devotion · stage: advanced · types: death-dying, karma-liberation_

`sourced` · confidence high

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, death-dying_

### 8.3-4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-3-4"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The Imperishable is the supreme Brahman; its own nature is called adhyātma; the creative offering that brings beings into existence is called action; adhibhūta is perishable existence; the Puruṣa is the adhidaivata; and I myself am the adhiyajña here in the body.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate_

`sourced` · confidence high

Those who know that the day of Brahmā lasts a thousand ages and his night a thousand ages know day and night; at the coming of day all manifest things arise from the unmanifest, and at the coming of night they dissolve into that same unmanifest; this same multitude of beings, arising again and again, dissolves helplessly at the coming of night and arises at the coming of day.

_level: conventional · standpoint: cosmic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: world-fate_

### 8.20-22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-20-22"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Beyond this unmanifest there is another eternal unmanifest being which does not perish when all beings perish; it is called the Imperishable and is the supreme goal, reaching which they do not return — that is my supreme abode; that supreme Puruṣa, in whom beings abide and by whom all this is pervaded, is to be won by undivided devotion.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, karma-liberation_

### 8.23-26 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-8-23-26"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

I will tell the times at which yogins depart to return or not to return: fire, light, day, the bright fortnight, the six months of the sun's northern course — departing then the knowers of Brahman go to Brahman; smoke, night, the dark fortnight, the six months of the southern course — departing then the yogin reaches the lunar light and returns; these two paths of the world, the bright and the dark, are held to be eternal: by one a person goes to non-return, by the other he returns again.

_level: conventional · standpoint: cosmic · path: general · stage: all · types: death-dying, world-fate_

### 9.7-10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-9-7-10"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

At the end of a kalpa all beings pass into my prakṛti, and at the beginning of a kalpa I send them forth again; resting on my own prakṛti I send forth again and again this whole multitude of beings, helpless under the power of prakṛti; these actions do not bind me, seated as one indifferent and unattached to them; with me as overseer, prakṛti brings forth the moving and the unmoving, and by this cause the world revolves.

`sourced` · confidence high

The catalogue of manifestations: among the Ādityas Kṛṣṇa is Viṣṇu, among lights the sun; among the Vedas the Sāmaveda, among the senses the mind, in beings consciousness (cetanā); among the Rudras Śaṃkara; among words the one syllable, among sacrifices the sacrifice of silent recitation (japa); among immovables the Himālaya, among trees the aśvattha, among the perfected the sage Kapila; among sciences the knowledge of the self, among letters the letter A; he is imperishable Time and all-seizing death; among the Vṛṣṇis Vāsudeva, among the Pāṇḍavas Arjuna, among sages Vyāsa, among poets Uśanas; of secrets he is silence, and the knowledge of knowers.

### 10.39-42 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-10-39-42"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Whatever is the seed of all beings, that I am; there is no being, moving or unmoving, that could exist without me; there is no end to my divine manifestations — what I have told is only an illustration; whatever being has glory, grace or power, know that it has sprung from a fragment of my splendour; but what need have you of this detailed knowledge? I stand, supporting this whole world with a single fragment of myself.

`sourced` · confidence high

Sañjaya describes the form: many mouths and eyes, many wondrous sights, divine ornaments and raised divine weapons, divine garlands and garments and perfumes, all-wonderful, infinite, facing every way; if the light of a thousand suns were to rise at once in the sky it might resemble the splendour of that great being; there Arjuna saw the whole universe with its many divisions gathered in one in the body of the god of gods; overcome with wonder, his hair standing on end, he bowed his head and spoke with joined palms.

### 12.13-20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-12-13-20"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The devotee dear to the Lord: without hatred toward any being, friendly and compassionate, without 'mine' and ego, the same in pleasure and pain, forgiving; ever content, the yogin, self-controlled, firm in resolve, mind and understanding dedicated to the Lord; one by whom the world is not agitated and who is not agitated by the world, free from elation, anger, fear and anxiety; without expectation, pure, skilful, unconcerned, untroubled, renouncing all undertakings; neither rejoicing nor hating, neither grieving nor desiring, renouncing good and evil; the same to foe and friend, in honour and dishonour, cold and heat, pleasure and pain, free from attachment; the same in blame and praise, silent, content with whatever comes, without fixed home, steady of mind, full of devotion; and those who with faith, holding the Lord supreme, follow this nectar of dharma are exceedingly dear to him.

### 13.1-2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-1-2"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

### 13.3-4 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-3-4"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Hear from me briefly what the field is, of what kind, with what modifications and whence, and who the knower is and what his powers; this has been sung by the seers in many ways, in distinct Vedic metres, and in the well-reasoned and decisive words of the Brahma-sūtra.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission_

### 13.5-6 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-5-6"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: body-layers, consciousness-mind_

### 13.7-11 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-7-11"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

What is called knowledge: absence of pride and pretence, non-harm, patience, uprightness, service of the teacher, purity, steadiness, self-restraint; dispassion toward sense-objects, absence of ego, seeing the evil of birth, death, old age, disease and pain; non-attachment, not clinging to son, wife and home, constant evenness of mind in the desired and the undesired; unswerving devotion to me with undivided yoga, resorting to solitary places, distaste for the company of crowds; constancy in the knowledge of the self and insight into the goal of knowledge of the truth — this is declared to be knowledge; what is contrary is ignorance.

`sourced` · confidence high

> jñeyaṃ yat tat pravakṣyāmi yaj jñātvāmṛtam aśnute anādimat paraṃ brahma na sat tan nāsad ucyate

terms: [brahman](../terms/brahman.md)

### 13.13-17 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-13-17"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

With hands and feet everywhere, eyes, heads and faces everywhere, with ears everywhere, it stands enveloping everything in the world; shining through the functions of all the senses yet free of all senses, unattached yet supporting all, free of the guṇas yet experiencing the guṇas; outside and inside beings, unmoving and moving, too subtle to be known, far and yet near; undivided yet standing as if divided among beings, the supporter of beings, their devourer and creator; the light of lights, said to be beyond darkness, knowledge, the knowable and the goal of knowledge, seated in the heart of all.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: apophatic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: ultimate_

terms: [brahman](../terms/brahman.md) · concepts: [The Lord in the heart of all beings](../concepts/lord-in-the-heart.md)

`sourced` · confidence high

### 13.19-20 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-19-20"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Know that prakṛti and puruṣa are both beginningless, and that the modifications and the guṇas arise from prakṛti; prakṛti is said to be the cause in the production of effect, instrument and agency, and puruṣa the cause in the experiencing of pleasure and pain.

> prakṛtiṃ puruṣaṃ caiva viddhy anādī ubhāv api vikārāṃś ca guṇāṃś caiva viddhi prakṛtisaṃbhavān kāryakāraṇakartṛtve hetuḥ prakṛtir ucyate puruṣaḥ sukhaduḥkhānāṃ bhoktṛtve hetur ucyate

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate_

### 13.19-23 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-13-19-23"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Know that prakṛti and puruṣa are both beginningless, and that the modifications and guṇas are born of prakṛti; prakṛti is called the cause in the producing of effect, instrument and agency, puruṣa the cause in the experiencing of pleasure and pain; puruṣa, seated in prakṛti, experiences the guṇas born of prakṛti, and attachment to the guṇas is the cause of his births in good and evil wombs; the supreme Puruṣa in this body is called the witness, the permitter, the supporter, the experiencer, the great Lord and the supreme self; one who thus knows puruṣa and prakṛti with its guṇas is not born again, however he may live.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, karma-liberation_

### 14.6-8 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-14-6-8"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Sattva, being stainless, illuminating and free of disease, binds by attachment to happiness and to knowledge; rajas, whose nature is passion, arising from thirst and attachment, binds the embodied one by attachment to action; tamas, born of ignorance and deluding all embodied beings, binds by negligence, sloth and sleep.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

`sourced` · confidence high

Sattva attaches to happiness, rajas to action, while tamas, veiling knowledge, attaches to negligence; sattva prevails by overpowering rajas and tamas, rajas by overpowering sattva and tamas, and tamas by overpowering sattva and rajas.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

`sourced` · confidence high

`sourced` · confidence high

`sourced` · confidence high

He does not hate illumination, activity and delusion when they arise, nor long for them when they cease; seated as one indifferent, he is not disturbed by the guṇas and, knowing that it is the guṇas that act, stands firm and does not waver; the same in pleasure and pain, abiding in the self, regarding a clod, a stone and gold as equal, the same toward the pleasant and the unpleasant, steady, the same in blame and praise; the same in honour and dishonour, to friend and foe, renouncing all undertakings — he is said to have gone beyond the guṇas.

### 16.9-18 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-16-9-18"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Holding this view, these lost souls of small understanding and cruel deeds rise up as enemies of the world for its destruction; given to insatiable desire, full of hypocrisy, pride and arrogance, they act with impure resolves; beset by innumerable cares ending only with death, they think: 'this I have gained today, this desire I shall fulfil; this enemy I have slain and others I shall slay; I am the lord, I am the enjoyer, I am successful, powerful and happy'; self-conceited, they sacrifice in name only, with ostentation and not according to rule, and hate me in their own bodies and in others'.

### 17.7-10 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-17-7-10"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Food dear to all is threefold, as are sacrifice, austerity and giving: foods that increase life, vitality, strength, health, happiness and cheerfulness, that are savoury, oily, substantial and agreeable, are dear to the sāttvic; foods that are bitter, sour, salty, very hot, pungent, dry and burning, producing pain, grief and disease, are desired by the rājasic; food that is stale, tasteless, putrid, left over from the previous day, remnants and impure, is dear to the tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, body-layers_

`sourced` · confidence high

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

### 17.17-19 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-17-17-19"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

This threefold austerity practised with supreme faith by yoked people who desire no fruit is sāttvic; austerity practised for respect, honour and reverence, with hypocrisy, is rājasic, unstable and fleeting; austerity practised with a foolish obstinacy, by torturing oneself, or to destroy another, is tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: general · stage: all · types: practice, ethics_

`sourced` · confidence high

Therefore the acts of sacrifice, giving and austerity enjoined by scripture are always begun by the expounders of Brahman with the utterance of 'Oṃ'; with 'tat', without aiming at the fruit, the seekers of liberation perform acts of sacrifice, austerity and giving; 'sat' is used in the sense of reality and of goodness, and for a praiseworthy act; steadfastness in sacrifice, austerity and giving is also called 'sat', as is any action for that purpose.

### 18.20-22 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-20-22"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Knowledge by which one sees one imperishable being in all beings, undivided in the divided, is sāttvic; knowledge that sees in all beings manifold separate entities of different kinds is rājasic; knowledge that clings to one effect as if it were the whole, without reason, without grasp of truth and trifling, is tāmasic.

> sarvabhūteṣu yenaikaṃ bhāvam avyayam īkṣate avibhaktaṃ vibhakteṣu taj jñānaṃ viddhi sāttvikam pṛthaktvena tu yaj jñānaṃ nānābhāvān pṛthagvidhān vetti sarveṣu bhūteṣu taj jñānaṃ viddhi rājasam yat tu kṛtsnavad ekasmin kārye saktam ahaitukam atattvārthavad alpaṃ ca tat tāmasam udāhṛtam

### 18.23-25 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-23-25"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: action · stage: all · types: ethics, practice_

### 18.26-28 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-26-28"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

An agent free from attachment, without egoism, endowed with steadiness and zeal, unchanged in success and failure, is sāttvic; one who is passionate, desires the fruit of action, greedy, harmful, impure and moved by joy and sorrow is rājasic; one who is unyoked, vulgar, obstinate, deceitful, malicious, lazy, despondent and procrastinating is tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: action · stage: all · types: ethics_

concepts: [The guṇa-classification of knowledge, action, agent, intellect, steadfastness and happiness](../concepts/guna-typology-of-gita-18.md)

### 18.29-32 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-29-32"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The understanding that knows the way of action and of withdrawal, what is to be done and not done, what is to be feared and not feared, bondage and liberation, is sāttvic; the understanding that knows dharma and adharma, what is to be done and not, wrongly, is rājasic; the understanding that, enveloped in darkness, takes adharma for dharma and sees all things perversely, is tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md) · concepts: [The guṇa-classification of knowledge, action, agent, intellect, steadfastness and happiness](../concepts/guna-typology-of-gita-18.md)

### 18.33-35 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-33-35"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The steadfastness (dhṛti) by which, through unswerving yoga, one holds the activities of mind, breath and senses is sāttvic; that by which one holds to dharma, pleasure and wealth with attachment, desiring the fruit, is rājasic; that by which a fool does not let go of sleep, fear, grief, dejection and conceit is tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [dhṛti](../terms/dhrti.md) · concepts: [The guṇa-classification of knowledge, action, agent, intellect, steadfastness and happiness](../concepts/guna-typology-of-gita-18.md)

### 18.36-39 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-36-39"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

Happiness in which one comes to delight through practice and reaches the end of sorrow — which is like poison at first but like nectar in the end, born of the serenity of one's own understanding — is sāttvic; happiness from the contact of the senses with their objects, like nectar at first but like poison in the end, is rājasic; happiness that deludes the self at the beginning and at the end, arising from sleep, sloth and negligence, is tāmasic.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

### 18.41-48 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-18-41-48"></a>
`sourced` · confidence high

The actions of brāhmaṇas, kṣatriyas, vaiśyas and śūdras are distributed according to the guṇas arising from their own nature: calm, self-restraint, austerity, purity, patience, uprightness, knowledge, realization and faith are the brāhmaṇa's; heroism, vigour, steadiness, skill, not fleeing in battle, generosity and lordliness the kṣatriya's; agriculture, cattle-keeping and trade the vaiśya's; action consisting of service the śūdra's; each devoted to his own action attains perfection, worshipping by his own action him from whom beings arise and by whom all this is pervaded; better one's own dharma though imperfect than another's well performed; doing the action prescribed by one's own nature one incurs no sin; one should not give up the action born with one, though faulty, for all undertakings are enveloped by fault as fire by smoke.

### ch1 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-ch1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Chapter 1 sets the scene and the crisis. 1.1: Dhṛtarāṣṭra asks Sañjaya what the two assembled armies did at Kurukṣetra, 'the field of dharma'. 1.2–11: Duryodhana goes to his teacher Droṇa, lists the leading warriors of the Pāṇḍava army (1.4–6) and of his own (1.8–9), compares the two forces (1.10) and orders that Bhīṣma be guarded (1.11). 1.12–19: Bhīṣma and then the Pāṇḍava side (Kṛṣṇa, Arjuna, Bhīma, Yudhiṣṭhira, Nakula, Sahadeva and others) blow their named conches; the uproar rends the Kauravas' hearts. 1.20–27: Arjuna asks Kṛṣṇa to station the chariot between the armies; Kṛṣṇa does so and tells him to look at the assembled Kurus; Arjuna sees his elders, teachers and kin on both sides. 1.28–46: overcome by pity (kṛpā) and despair (viṣāda), Arjuna describes his bodily collapse (1.28–30), says he sees no good (śreyas) in killing kin and wants neither victory nor kingship (1.31–37), argues that destroying a family destroys the family dharmas, leads to varṇa-mixture, the fall of the ancestors and hell (1.38–44), and says it would be better to be killed unresisting (1.45–46). 1.47: Arjuna sits down in the chariot, casting away his bow, his mind overwhelmed by grief (śoka). The chapter's purpose, as it presents itself, is to state Arjuna's grief and his argument from family dharma, which the following teaching answers.

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general · stage: unmarked · types: narrative, ethics_

terms: [dharma](../terms/dharma.md), [kuladharma](../terms/kuladharma.md), [varṇasaṅkara](../terms/varnasankara.md), [śoka](../terms/soka.md), [viṣāda](../terms/visada.md), [kṛpā](../terms/krpa.md) · concepts: [Family dharma (kula-dharma)](../concepts/kuladharma.md), [Mixture of the varṇas (varṇa-saṅkara)](../concepts/varnasankara.md) · teachers: [Dhṛtarāṣṭra](../teachers/dhrtarastra.md), [Sañjaya](../teachers/sanjaya.md), [Duryodhana](../teachers/duryodhana.md), [Droṇa](../teachers/drona.md), [Bhīṣma (Devavrata, Gāṅgeya)](../teachers/bhisma.md), [Kṛṣṇa (Vāsudeva)](../teachers/krsna.md), [Arjuna (Pārtha, Dhanaṃjaya)](../teachers/arjuna.md) · disputes: [Should Arjuna fight the battle in which his elders, teachers and kinsmen stand on the other side?](../debates/bhagavad-gita-arjuna-should-fight.md)

### ch2 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-ch2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Chapter 2 opens the teaching. 2.1–10 (frame): Kṛṣṇa rebukes Arjuna's faintness and unmanliness (2.2–3); Arjuna restates his dilemma — he cannot fight Bhīṣma and Droṇa, would rather live on alms, does not know which outcome is better — and asks Kṛṣṇa, as his disciple who has come for refuge, to tell him for certain what is better (śreyas) (2.4–8); he says 'I will not fight' and falls silent, and Kṛṣṇa, as if smiling, answers (2.9–10). 2.11–30: the teaching on the embodied self (dehin): the wise do not grieve; the self was never not and will never cease; it passes through bodies as through the ages of life and as through garments; what is not (asat) has no being and what is (sat) no non-being; the self neither slays nor is slain, is unborn, eternal, uncuttable, unmanifest and unthinkable; even on the contrary view birth and death are inevitable; therefore do not grieve. 2.31–38: the argument from one's own dharma (svadharma) and honour: a dharmic battle is the kṣatriya's best good and a door to heaven, refusal brings sin and disgrace; fight treating pleasure and pain, gain and loss, victory and defeat alike. 2.39: transition — the understanding (buddhi) given 'in sāṃkhya' is now given 'in yoga'. 2.40–53: the yoga of understanding (buddhi-yoga): resolute understanding is one (2.41); the flowery speech of those who delight in Vedic statements and seek heaven and power keeps understanding from samādhi; the Vedas concern the three guṇas, be free of them (2.42–46); your right is to action, not its fruits; yoga is sameness and skill in actions; the wise, yoked with understanding, abandon the fruit and are freed from the bondage of birth (2.47–51); when understanding passes beyond delusion and stands steady in samādhi, you will attain yoga (2.52–53). 2.54–72: Arjuna asks about the person of steady wisdom (sthitaprajña); Kṛṣṇa describes him: he gives up all desires and is content in the self, unshaken by sorrow and pleasure, free from passion, fear and anger, withdraws the senses like a tortoise (2.55–61); the chain from dwelling on objects through attachment, desire, anger, delusion and loss of memory to ruin (2.62–63), and its reverse through mastered senses to serenity and steady understanding (2.64–68); the sage is awake where beings sleep, is filled by desires like the ocean yet unmoved, and without 'mine' and 'I' attains peace (2.69–71); this is the brahmic state, reaching which even at death one attains brahma-nirvāṇa (2.72). The chapter's purpose, as it presents itself, is to answer Arjuna's grief and his request for the good, first by knowledge of the self, then by the discipline of action done with steady understanding, culminating in the portrait of the one whose wisdom is steady.

### ch3 <a id="tea-bhagavad-gita-ch3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Chapter 3 answers the question of understanding versus action. 3.1–2: Arjuna asks why, if understanding (buddhi) is held superior to action, Kṛṣṇa urges him to terrible action, and asks for one decisive teaching. 3.3–9: Kṛṣṇa says he declared of old a twofold commitment (niṣṭhā) — the yoga of knowledge for the sāṃkhyas and the yoga of action for the yogins; actionlessness (naiṣkarmya) is not reached by not acting nor perfection by mere renunciation; no one can stay without acting, since the guṇas of prakṛti make everyone act; restraining the organs of action while the mind dwells on objects is hypocrisy, while karma-yoga with the senses controlled and without attachment excels; do your allotted action, for the world is bound by action except action for sacrifice. 3.10–16: Prajāpati created beings with sacrifice, telling them to nourish the gods and be nourished; who enjoys the gods' gifts without offering is a thief; eating the remains of sacrifice frees from fault; the wheel of food, rain, sacrifice, action, brahman and the imperishable must be kept turning. 3.17–18: for one who delights and is content in the self there is nothing that must be done. 3.19–26: therefore act unattached; Janaka and others reached perfection by action; act for the holding together of the world (loka-saṃgraha), since people follow the best; Kṛṣṇa himself, needing nothing, keeps acting lest the worlds perish; the wise should act unattached as the unwise act attached, and not unsettle the understanding of the ignorant. 3.27–29: actions are done by the guṇas, and only one deluded by the sense of 'I' (ahaṃkāra) thinks 'I am the doer'; the knower of the truth is not attached. 3.30–32: resign all actions to Kṛṣṇa and fight; those who follow this teaching with faith are released, those who cavil are lost. 3.33–35: all beings follow their nature; attraction and aversion lie in wait in the sense-objects; one's own dharma, though imperfect, is better than another's. 3.36–43: Arjuna asks what drives a person to sin against his will; Kṛṣṇa names desire and anger, born of rajas, the enemy that covers knowledge as smoke covers fire, seated in the senses, mind and understanding; restrain the senses first, know what is beyond understanding, steady the self by the self and slay desire. The chapter's purpose, as it presents itself, is to show why Arjuna must act — action being unavoidable, sacrificial, exemplary and non-binding when done without attachment — and to name desire as what must be overcome.

Verse counts per chapter checked against the local corpus; ch. 13's extra opening verse (Arjuna asking about prakṛti, puruṣa, kṣetra, kṣetrajña, jñāna and jñeya) is absent from the Critical Edition, and this unit uses the 700-verse numbering throughout._
